# STEA-004 / ACMS Next Feature Implementation Plan
## Backend Hygiene -> Durable IDs / Artifacts / Projects / Cost / Fleet -> Operator UI

**Date:** 2026-10-01  
**Executor:** STEA-004 Entrepreneur or successor implementation agent  
**Systems:** ACMS CT122, LLM Manager / Server Manager VM114, Agent Bridge / Hermes worker fleet, Jira, Service Registry / Homarr  
**Plan type:** next production feature cycle after successful ACMS -> A2A -> Hermes -> LLM Manager acceptance

---

# 0. BLUF

The production execution path works.

Do **not** rebuild it.

Current verified foundation:

```text
Jira -> ACMS reconciliation                    WORKING
ACMS -> A2A dispatch                           WORKING
A2A ACK / one-task lock / idempotency         WORKING
Hermes execution                              WORKING
Qwen3.8-Flash-Next local inference            WORKING
Heartbeat fleet 5/5                           WORKING
Live event/SSE backend                        WORKING
Artifact backend                              WORKING
Products / Projects backend                   WORKING
Inbox backend                                 WORKING
Model-policy precedence                       WORKING
Emporia facility collector backend            WORKING
```

This phase is primarily:

```text
1. Fix backend hygiene first.
2. Fix human IDs, handoffs, Jira-template fidelity, Projects/Products, costs, artifacts, Fleet.
3. Make ACMS operator UI useful.
```

Do functionality first.

Do not spend the implementation window on visual decoration.

---

# 1. User-Priority Execution Order

Execute in this order.

```text
A1. VM123 safe deletion
A2. golden-template 121 repair

```

# 3. Phase A1 - Delete VM123 Safely

Human authorization is explicit:

```text
VM123 may be deleted.
```

Current problem:

```text
VM123 is stopped duplicate acms-worker-001
and contains static .203 networking.
Starting it risks collision with live VM124.
```

Before destruction:

```text
qm config 123
confirm node
confirm stopped
confirm no unique attached storage
confirm no unique snapshots required
confirm no ACMS runtime record references VM123
confirm no Registry record marks VM123 as active
confirm no backup/rollback requirement remains
```

Capture final config:

```text
raw/VM123-qm-config-before-delete.txt
```

If there is unique disk/state:

```text
STOP
document
do not delete
```

Otherwise destroy VM123 without starting it.

Verify:

```text
VM123 no longer exists
VM124 remains healthy
10.0.20.203 owned only by VM124
uid-001 heartbeat remains fresh
```

---

# 4. Phase A2 - Repair Golden Worker Template 121

The template must stop creating broken clones.

Current known defects:

```text
static 10.0.20.203 baked in
clone identity cleanup required manually
Hermes runtime incomplete
aiohttp missing
bridge bootstrap incomplete
```

Template 121 must contain:

```text
supported base OS
QEMU guest agent
Python/runtime requirements
Hermes Agent
aiohttp
bridge bootstrap code
systemd bridge template/unit support
cloud-init support
no worker-specific API keys
no worker-specific LLM Manager keys
no static worker IP
no copied SSH host keys
no copied machine-id
no copied Agent UID
```

Clone-time data must include:

```text
hostname
worker UID
ACMS agent UUID association
VMID
network configuration
IP / DHCP reservation
Hermes profile name
unique bridge API key
unique LLM Manager agent key
model policy
```

Never create a clone that boots on the network with another worker's IP.

Preferred flow:

```text
clone powered off
-> assign VMID/node/storage
-> inject unique cloud-init/network configuration
-> regenerate machine identity
-> regenerate SSH host keys
-> inject non-secret worker metadata
-> provision secrets securely
-> first boot
-> health check
-> bridge registration
-> ACMS heartbeat
```

Acceptance:

```text
unique machine-id
unique host keys
no .203
aiohttp present
Hermes launches
bridge launches
no secrets inherited
QGA works
network works
```

Use one disposable test clone, then destroy it.

Do not create worker-006 unless separately authorized.

# 5. Phase A3 - Enforce Server Manager Placement Policy

Placement must become code, not convention.

General workers:

```text
MIAM00100 preferred
```

Inference workers:

```text
maximum 1 worker per inference node
```

Known inference nodes:

```text
MIAM00111
MIAM00112
MIAM00143
MIAM00144
```

Previously approved critical exclusions:

```text
MIAM00133
MIAM00147
```

Hard filters:

```text
node healthy
not excluded
storage available
RAM above threshold
CPU below threshold
not already at worker cap
```

Preference:

```text
MIAM00100 general-worker bonus
non-inference node preferred after MIAM00100
inference node large penalty
inference node rejected if it already has one worker
```

Human Proxmox migration remains valid.

After a human migrates a worker:

```text
durable Agent UID unchanged
ACMS UUID unchanged
runtime node/VM/IP reconciled
```

# 6. Human-Readable Work Item UID

Add immutable human UID:

```text
ACMS-WORK-######-YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS
```

Example:

```text
ACMS-WORK-000127-20261001_082241
```

Rules:

```text
###### = global monotonic 6-digit work sequence
timestamp = Work Item created_at
timezone for UID = UTC
UID immutable after creation
internal UUID remains immutable database identity
```

Use a concurrency-safe DB sequence/allocation mechanism.

Do not derive by `COUNT(*)`.

Backfill existing Work Items by:

```text
created_at ASC
then internal UUID ASC
```

Never reuse a sequence.

Human UI uses Work UID as primary link text.

Internal UUID goes under Technical Details.

---

# 7. Human-Readable Artifact UID

Add immutable human UID:

```text
ACMS-ARTIFACT-######-YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS
```

Example:

```text
ACMS-ARTIFACT-000042-20261001_082612
```

Rules match Work UID:

```text
global monotonic sequence
created_at UTC timestamp
internal UUID preserved
sha256 preserved
```

Backfill deterministically.

# 9. One Markdown Handoff Per Completed Work Item

Inbox is not the handoff.

For every terminal Work Item, create/finalize one canonical Markdown handoff:

```text
artifact_type = work_handoff
```

For successful work, it is mandatory.

If agent provided a handoff:

```text
use agent handoff as canonical content
```

If no handoff exists:

```text
ACMS generates a concise fallback handoff from durable events
```

Minimum structure:

```markdown
# Work Handoff - <Work UID>

## BLUF

## Requested Work

## What Was Done

## Result

## Tests / Evidence

## Model / Agent / Runtime

## Artifacts / Code / PRs

## Jira

## Costs / Usage

## Problems / Warnings

## Remaining Work
```

Work detail must prominently show:

```text
HANDOFF
Artifact UID
View Markdown
Download
Copy link
```

Do not force humans to manually fill an empty Add Handoff form after successful automated work.

# 10. Correct STNA-87 / Jira Template Fidelity

The previous acceptance ticket did not faithfully use STNA-86.

Do not repeat that.

For historical STNA-87:

```text
preserve ticket
preserve evidence
add correction note if useful
link its canonical ACMS handoff artifact explicitly
```

For future explicitly authorized AI-created Jira tickets:

```text
CLONE STNA-86
```

Do not approximate the template from memory.

Preserve current template structure.

Validate expected sections before moving to `TO START`.

If Jira cloning is unavailable:

```text
fetch STNA-86 current fields/description
create structurally equivalent ticket
compare sections
fail if required sections are absent
```

No ordinary ACMS autonomous ticket creation.

# 13. Project Data Model / UI

Projects are durable context pages.

Every Work Item belongs to a Project.

Projects may exist without Jira.

Expected common case:

```text
1 Jira ticket -> 1 Project
```

but do not enforce strict one-to-one at DB level because Projects can exist without Jira and future Projects may span multiple Jira issues.

Project page shows:

```text
Project name / UID
Product association
repositories
Jira mappings
Sprint mappings
tags
model preference
current work
completed work
artifacts
Inbox items
recent decisions/context
recent agent activity
cost/usage
```

Do not create one Project per Work Item automatically.

# 16. Slop Ratio Metrics

Metric name:

```text
Slop Ratio
```

For each Product show two ratios.

## 16.1 True Slop Ratio

```text
True Slop Ratio
=
Lines of Code
/
Human-written ADRs
```

For v1, count an ADR as "human-written" when it contains or links at least one durable human-authored decision / answered question.

Store separately:

```text
human_written_adr_count
human_answer_count
```

This allows later refinement.

## 16.2 Precision Slop Ratio

```text
Precision Slop Ratio
=
Lines of Code
/
Total written ADRs
```

Interpretation:

```text
Too high:
AI may be building too much without enough human direction.

Too low:
humans may be spending too much time discussing/documenting instead of building.
```

Do not hard-code good/bad thresholds yet.

Collect baseline history first.

## LOC rules

Use a language-aware counter.

Exclude:

```text
.git
vendor
node_modules
venv
generated build output
vendored third-party code
```

Document exact exclusions.

## ADR rules

Count accepted/current ADR documents.

Do not count templates or deleted docs.

## Multi-repository Product

```text
Product LOC = sum unique associated repository LOC
```

Do not double-count one repo linked to multiple Projects under one Product.

Apply same unique-source rule to ADR counts.

## Refresh

Default:

```text
hourly
```

Also provide:

```text
Refresh now
```

Store timeseries:

```text
timestamp
LOC
human ADR count
total ADR count
True Slop Ratio
Precision Slop Ratio
commit SHAs
```

Show history chart.

# 21. Hermes Chat UI - Use Harness Commands

The ACMS agent conversation UI should behave like Telegram / ChatGPT.

Do not build a custom Steer panel or custom interactive model panel for normal chat.

Use harness-native commands.

## Chat input

One normal input box.

Support:

```text
normal text
slash commands
file upload
```

For Hermes:

```text
/model
/new
/status
other supported Hermes commands
```

Pass commands through Agent Bridge to Hermes.

## Slash-command autocomplete

When user types:

```text
/
```

show harness-advertised commands.

Typing:

```text
/mo
```

should suggest:

```text
/model
```

Do not hard-code Hermes-only commands into generic ACMS core without harness capability metadata.

Future harnesses may expose different commands.

## Model defaults

Persistent backend model policy remains useful.

Interactive switching should use harness commands where practical.

Do not duplicate functionality unnecessarily.

Avoid huge logs in default view.

Home \"not yet implemented\"\n  list must track what the backend actually maintains.\n- **Tooling-self-replacement caveat:** when a target tip CHANGES\n  `deploy/release.sh` (or rollback/validate scripts), the running transaction\n  checks out the new tree mid-flight while bash still reads the old script\n  from disk — unproven territory. Resolve\n  with `shutil.which(\"alembic\") or str(REPO / \".venv-acms\" / \"bin\" / \"alembic\")` (the pattern now\n  used in all migration/integration test helpers).\n- The maintenance-mode nginx conf overlays the tracked `nginx.conf`; any `git checkout` on the VM must restore that file first or checkout is refused. `deploy/common.sh` handles this — keep the pattern if touching release tooling.\n- Never run `alembic downgrade` automatically (plan §29); destructive migrations need human approval + restore testing.\n- Level-2 DB restore is only authorized inside an open release transaction (`transaction.json`); standalone rollback is app-only by design.\n- FastAPI + TestClient infinite-stream hang: an SSE endpoint (`while True` +\n  `is_disconnected()`) never returns through TestClient and hangs the whole\n  suite. Fix in ENDPOINT code, not the test: accept a bounded `max_events`\n  query param; when bounded, exit the generator once the drain target is met\n  without awaiting the next keepalive sleep (a sleep-before-check ordering\n  still hangs when zero events match). Also gives an operational safety cap.\n  ACMS SSE (2026-09-28) uses: semantic allow-list filtering, sequence-ordered\n  durable-log source, `Last-Event-ID` exactly-once resume, `X-Accel-Buffering:\n  no` for nginx.\n- `ALTER TYPE … ADD VALUE IF NOT EXISTS` needs a real migration for any new PG\n  enum value (SUPERSEDED runtime state, migration 0003, 2026-09-28) — SQLite\n  passes without it, so only the PG integration test catches it. Budget\n  gate lessons (PR #28): gate lives in the single authoritative dispatch\n  service (`dispatch_service.py`, `POST /api/v1/dispatch/work/{id}`), not UI/\n  controller layers; unknown cost is UNKNOWN, never coerced to zero;\n  idempotency via `disp-<sha256>` external_task_id; `add_event` takes\n  work_key/assignment_key, not item ids.\n- **Shared SQLite unit DB persists across tests in one run** — state-sensitive\n  suites (economics, attention, dispatch) need the `clean_db` conftest fixture\n  (DELETE FROM all tables) or they see prior tests' rows. Migration-test\n  ordering: tests sharing `/tmp/acms-integration-pgdata` pollute alembic_version\n  — pin exact revisions (`upgrade 0007_work_budgets`, not `head`) and start\n  from `downgrade base` when asserting a specific stamp.\n- Live CT122 layout facts (repo path, container names, allowlist, DNS reality) → see `references/ct122-live-layout.md` before running anything on the box.\n\n## Related skills\n\n- `implementation-package-execution` — for zip+plan package deliveries (overlaps on STOP gates/handoffs)\n- `server-rendered-ui` — the Jinja2 UI conventions ADR-0008 builds on\n- `github-pr-workflow` — generic PR lifecycle (protected; org quirks live here, not there)\n", "path": "devops/acms-project-framework/SKILL.md", "skill_dir": "/home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/skills/devops/acms-project-framework", "linked_files": {"references": ["references/ct122-live-layout.md"]}, "usage_hint": "To view linked files, call skill_view(name, file_path) where file_path is e.g. 'references/api.md' or 'assets/config.yaml'", "required_environment_variables": [], "required_commands": [], "missing_required_environment_variables": [], "missing_credential_files": [], "missing_required_commands": [], "setup_needed": false, "setup_skipped": false, "readiness_status": "available"}

Class-level workflow for 'unzip this and run the plan' handoffs (ACMS, llm-manager-project-framework, future frameworks).", "tags": ["GitHub", "Packages", "Handoffs", "PRs", "Validation", "agents-md"], "related_skills": ["github-pr-workflow", "deployed-service-capture", "github-repo-management"], "content": "---\nname: implementation-package-execution\ndescription: \"Execute a user-delivered implementation package (zip archive + instruction markdown) against a GitHub repo: extract, secret-scan, apply docs bootstrap and/or code overlay per the plan's steps, re-validate in the live repo, open PR, honor human-review STOP gates, log to agents.md. Class-level workflow for 'unzip this and run the plan' handoffs (ACMS, llm-manager-project-framework, future frameworks).\"\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [GitHub, Packages, Handoffs, PRs, Validation, agents-md]\n    related_skills: [github-pr-workflow, deployed-service-capture, github-repo-management]\n---\n\n# Execute a Delivered Implementation Package (zip + instructions → GitHub)\n\nUse when Jordan sends a zip (repo content / overlay trees) plus an instruction markdown (\"UPLOAD_AND_EXECUTE_…\", runbook, or plan) targeting a GitHub repo. The instruction file is the authoritative spec — follow its steps literally, **including its STOP/human-review gates** (e.g. \"Do not merge PR-001 autonomously\", \"human must approve ADR-0007 before merge\"). Two occurrences so far: LLM-Manager docs package and ACMS bootstrap + PR-001 (`startupteams/acms-project-framework`, 2026-09-25); both share the shape: one docs tree for `main`, one code overlay for a feature branch + PR.\n\n## Workflow\n\n1. **Extract & inventory.** `unzip` may not be installed — fall back to `python3 -c \"import zipfile; zipfile.ZipFile(p).extractall(d)\"`. List all files in every tree; read the instruction MD fully before acting.\n2. **Secret scan the whole package BEFORE any commit** (same rule as `deployed-service-capture`): grep for `sk-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{8,}`, DSNs `://…@`, `ghp_`/`github_pat_`, private-key headers, password/api-key lines. Filter out legitimate prose/placeholders (`change-me`, `test-token`, `replace-with-…`, `os.environ`). Report clean/hits to Jordan.\n3. **Check GitHub access + repo existence** (`gh auth status`, `git ls-remote`). If the target is under `startupteams` (or any org outside `jordatech`), get **explicit per-task authorization via clarify** before pushing — user-profile boundary. A direct-to-`main` bootstrap commit is allowed ONLY if the instruction file states it's human-authorized (one-time exception); all later feature work goes through branch + PR.\n   - **Issues often DISABLED on startupteams repos** (`gh issue create` → \"repository has disabled issues\"). Re-enable first: `gh api -X PATCH repos/<o>/<r> --input - <<< '{\"has_issues\": true}'` (jordatech token has repo scope) — happened on llm-manager (09-25) AND pdu-framework (09-27).\n   - **When the repo already has content**, diff the package against `main` BEFORE copying (`diff -rq pkg/ repo/`): the package may be a docs OVERLAY (6 files modify existing files + N new) rather than a bootstrap. Stage only what the overlay intends; delivery metadata files (`*_MANIFEST.md`, `PR_BODY.md`, the instruction MD itself) stay OUT of the repo.\n4. **Clone fresh** to `~/work/<repo>` (don't reuse stale clones). Apply each tree per the plan's `cp -a \"<tree>/.\" \"$REPO_DIR/\"`, stage **only the file lists the plan specifies**, commit with the plan's message, push.\n5. **Re-run validation in the live repo before opening the PR**, even if the plan says validation already passed on the prepared overlay. Match the project's `requires-python` (check `pyproject.toml`) — system python may be older; create a venv with the right interpreter (`/usr/bin/python3.12 -m venv .venv-<name>`), `pip install -e '.[dev]'`, run the test command from the plan.\n6. **Artifact-cleanliness gate before staging** (see Pitfalls).\n7. **Open the PR** with `gh pr create --body-file <package>/PR_BODY.md` — keep the PR body as a file in the extract dir, do NOT commit `PR_BODY.md` into the repo. Verify with `gh pr view <n> --json state,baseRefName,headRefName,additions,fileCount`.\n8. **Stop at the plan's human gate** — with one explicit exception: a session-scoped self-merge authorization from Jordan (stated in chat, e.g. \"for this session, automatically merge all the PRs you are doing\") applies to ROUTINE feature PRs of that sprint only. It does NOT extend to: (a) a plan's own explicit human-decision gates that name a specific decision (production cutover, ADR acceptance where the plan says \"human must approve\", infrastructure shutdown) — those stay human even under session auto-merge; (b) destructive actions, secrets, scope beyond the plan. When in doubt, treat shutdown-of-production / onboot-flip / credentials-requiring steps as human-gated. State clearly in the final reply which decisions are waiting on Jordan.\n9. **Log the milestone to `~/agents.md`** (repo, commits, PR link + number, gate status, any fixes made during execution, local paths) and update memory only with durable facts (repo authorization, clone location) — not PR numbers/commit SHAs as standalone memory entries; agents.md is the log.\n\n## Continuing/revising an OPEN PR across sessions\n\nWhen a plan says \"revise the existing PR, do not open a new one\" (e.g. the ACMS PR #1 async-stack revision after ADR-0007 acceptance):\n\n1. Re-derive state from the live repo first: `git status`, `git log --oneline`, `gh pr view N --json state,updatedAt`, and re-read the plan's stop conditions. Prior-session work may sit uncommitted in the working tree — validate it, don't redo it.\n2. Re-run the FULL validation suite in the live repo before committing (tests, stack-validation script, compile check, secret scan) — never trust the previous session's \"it passed\".\n3. Commit as focused follow-up commits on the same branch (`feat(...)` / `test(...)` / `docs(...)` split), push, and **update the PR body** to reflect the revision (see pitfall below — `gh pr edit` fails silently on this). Merge stays a human action.\n4. Write a new dated handoff doc in the repo (`docs/handoffs/<date>-<slug>.md`) with a validation table of exact executed commands + results, commit it on the branch, attach the path in the final reply.\n\n## Pitfalls\n\n- **`gh pr edit --body-file` silently fails on orgs with projects-classic**: errors with a \"Projects (classic) is being deprecated\" GraphQL message, exits non-zero, and the body is NOT updated. Use the REST fallback `gh api -X PATCH repos/OWNER/REPO/pulls/N --input -` (JSON `{\"body\": ...}` on stdin), then VERIFY with `gh pr view N --json body -q .body | grep -c \"<unique-marker>\"`.\n- **`&&` chains abort on a no-match grep.** `grep -c pattern file && git add ...` returns rc=1 when the count is 0, silently skipping the rest of the chain (a commit+push never ran and looked \"done\"). Run commits/pushes as their own commands, or append `|| true` to informational greps.\n- **Foreground terminal refuses inline scripts that spawn a server** (\"appears to start a long-lived server/watch process\") even when the script self-terminates. Write the script to a file (e.g. `scripts/validate_*.py`) and run it with `terminal(background=true, notify_on_complete=true)`; keep it printing a clear PASS line.\n- **No Docker needed to validate a real PostgreSQL stack**: the `pgserver` dev extra runs embedded PostgreSQL 16 — `pgserver.get_server(dir, cleanup_mode=\"delete\")` gives a URI for Alembic-upgrade-from-zero, uvicorn-subprocess HTTP checks, and after-exit durability queries. Recipe: `references/pg-stack-validation-no-docker.md`. Do NOT record \"docker is unavailable\" as an environment constraint; use this path.\n- **Accidental branch creation from defensive commands**: a guard like `git checkout -b <branch>-tmp 2>/dev/null` succeeds and leaves you on the temp branch with the same content — verify `git branch --show-current` before committing, and delete stray temp branches.\n- **CHECK `git branch --show-current` BEFORE EVERY COMMIT.** With long sessions + many branch switches, a docs commit can silently land on `main` (happened 2026-09-27 on acms: commit 9b5f0ff went straight to main; happened AGAIN the same day on pdu-marion: two separate direct-to-main slips — docs commit and a staging-fixes commit). Repair pattern if caught: `git branch <correct-branch> <sha>` → `git checkout main` → `git reset --hard <merge-base>` → `git push --force origin main` → push the branch → PR → merge. Fast, honest, history ends clean. Prevention: run `git branch --show-current && git log --oneline -1` as part of the commit command itself, and ALWAYS `git checkout -b <branch>` before the first edit of a change — even \"small doc fixes\". A first PR followed by more work is the highest-risk window: you're on `main` after merging and the next edit happens there.\n- **`git rm -r --cached dist/` fixes only the CURRENT branch's index — regenerable artifacts keep coming back.** If a build step (build-release.sh → dist/) runs locally, the next `git add -A` sweeps the artifacts in again on every new branch. Fix `.gitignore` on the FIRST occurrence (not just the index), and `git status -s | grep -v <artifact-dir>` before every commit.\n\n- **Build artifacts sneak into `git add` of directories.** Running pytest/pip-install inside the repo creates `__pycache__/`, `*.egg-info/`, `.venv*/`, local `*.db`; `git add acms tests` sweeps the .pyc files in even when the plan lists exact paths. Before committing: `git status --short --ignored`, check `.gitignore` covers `__pycache__/`, `*.py[cod]`, `.venv*/`, `*.egg-info/`, `*.db`; extend it (with `patch`/append, never overwrite) or delete artifacts first. If a commit already caught them: `git rm -r --cached <dirs>` + extend `.gitignore` + follow-up `chore:` commit on the same PR branch (don't rewrite pushed history).\n- **Instruction scripts assume `unzip`, a writable `$HOME/work`, and exact python availability** — substitute equivalents (python zipfile, explicit REPO_DIR, versioned interpreter + venv) rather than failing.\n- **Plan file-lists omit generated files you create while validating** (venv, egg-info from `pip install -e`). If `gh pr create` warns \"uncommitted change\", check whether it's untracked local junk (gitignore it) vs. genuinely missing content.\n- **Don't apply the whole package blindly at repo root** — the instruction MD itself and `PR_BODY.md` belong outside the tree (the plan's `git add` lists show exactly what enters the repo).\n- Overlay trees may MODIFY files already on `main` (bootstrap case): `git status` after `cp -a` shows ` M` entries — that's expected; still stage only the plan's listed paths.\n\n## Related\n\n- `deployed-service-capture` — sibling direction (live VM → repo); shares the two-pass secret-scan rule and `references/secret-scan-patterns.md` patterns there.\n- `github-pr-workflow` — generic PR lifecycle commands (gh and curl fallbacks).\n- `github-repo-management` — clone/remote basics.\n", "path": "devops/implementation-package-execution/SKILL.md", "skill_dir": "/home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/skills/devops/implementation-package-execution", "linked_files": null, "usage_hint": null, "required_environment_variables": [], "required_commands": [], "missing_required_environment_variables": [], "missing_credential_files": [], "missing_required_commands": [], "setup_needed": false, "setup_skipped": false, "readiness_status": "available", "metadata": {"hermes": {"tags": ["GitHub", "Packages", "Handoffs", "PRs", "Validation", "agents-md"], "related_skills": ["github-pr-workflow", "deployed-service-capture", "github-repo-management"]}}}

{"success": true, "name": "github-pr-workflow", "description": "GitHub PR lifecycle: branch, commit, open, CI, merge.", "tags": ["GitHub", "Pull-Requests", "CI/CD", "Git", "Automation", "Merge"], "related_skills": ["github-auth", "github-code-review"], "content": "---\nname: github-pr-workflow\ndescription: \"GitHub PR lifecycle: branch, commit, open, CI, merge.\"\nversion: 1.1.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent\nlicense: MIT\nplatforms: [linux, macos, windows]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [GitHub, Pull-Requests, CI/CD, Git, Automation, Merge]\n    related_skills: [github-auth, github-code-review]\n---\n\n# GitHub Pull Request Workflow\n\nComplete guide for managing the PR lifecycle. Each section shows the `gh` way first, then the `git` + `curl` fallback for machines without `gh`.\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- Authenticated with GitHub (see `github-auth` skill)\n- Inside a git repository with a GitHub remote\n\n### Quick Auth Detection\n\n```bash\n# Determine which method to use throughout this workflow\nif command -v gh &>/dev/null && gh auth status &>/dev/null; then\n  AUTH=\"gh\"\nelse\n  AUTH=\"git\"\n  # Ensure we have a token for API calls\n  if [ -z \"$GITHUB_TOKEN\" ]; then\n    if _hermes_env=\"${HERMES_HOME:-$HOME/.hermes}/.env\"; [ -f \"$_hermes_env\" ] && grep -q \"^GITHUB_TOKEN=\" \"$_hermes_env\"; then\n      GITHUB_TOKEN=$(grep \"^GITHUB_TOKEN=\" \"$_hermes_env\" | head -1 | cut -d= -f2 | tr -d '\\n\\r')\n    elif grep -q \"github.com\" ~/.git-credentials 2>/dev/null; then\n      GITHUB_TOKEN=$(grep \"github.com\" ~/.git-credentials 2>/dev/null | head -1 | sed 's|https://[^:]*:\\([^@]*\\)@.*|\\1|')\n    fi\n  fi\nfi\necho \"Using: $AUTH\"\n```\n\n### Extracting Owner/Repo from the Git Remote\n\nMany `curl` commands need `owner/repo`. Extract it from the git remote:\n\n```bash\n# Works for both HTTPS and SSH remote URLs\nREMOTE_URL=$(git remote get-url origin)\nOWNER_REPO=$(echo \"$REMOTE_URL\" | sed -E 's|.*github\\.com[:/]||; s|\\.git$||')\nOWNER=$(echo \"$OWNER_REPO\" | cut -d/ -f1)\nREPO=$(echo \"$OWNER_REPO\" | cut -d/ -f2)\necho \"Owner: $OWNER, Repo: $REPO\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## 1. Branch Creation\n\nThis part is pure `git` — identical either way:\n\n```bash\n# Make sure you're up to date\ngit fetch origin\ngit checkout main && git pull origin main\n\n# Create and switch to a new branch\ngit checkout -b feat/add-user-authentication\n```\n\nBranch naming conventions:\n- `feat/description` — new features\n- `fix/description` — bug fixes\n- `refactor/description` — code restructuring\n- `docs/description` — documentation\n- `ci/description` — CI/CD changes\n\n## 2. Making Commits\n\nUse the agent's file tools (`write_file`, `patch`) to make changes, then commit:\n\n```bash\n# Stage specific files\ngit add src/auth.py src/models/user.py tests/test_auth.py\n\n# Commit with a conventional commit message\ngit commit -m \"feat: add JWT-based user authentication\n\n- Add login/register endpoints\n- Add User model with password hashing\n- Add auth middleware for protected routes\n- Add unit tests for auth flow\"\n```\n\nCommit message format (Conventional Commits):\n```\ntype(scope): short description\n\nLonger explanation if needed. Wrap at 72 characters.\n```\n\nTypes: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `docs`, `test`, `ci`, `chore`, `perf`\n\n## 3. Pushing and Creating a PR\n\n### Push the Branch (same either way)\n\n```bash\ngit push -u origin HEAD\n```\n\n### Create the PR\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\ngh pr create \\\n  --title \"feat: add JWT-based user authentication\" \\\n  --body \"## Summary\n- Adds login and register API endpoints\n- JWT token generation and validation\n\n## Test Plan\n- [ ] Unit tests pass\n\nCloses #42\"\n```\n\nOptions: `--draft`, `--reviewer user1,user2`, `--label \"enhancement\"`, `--base develop`\n\n**With git + curl:**\n\n```bash\nBRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)\n\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  -H \"Accept: application/vnd.github.v3+json\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/pulls \\\n  -d \"{\n    \\\"title\\\": \\\"feat: add JWT-based user authentication\\\",\n    \\\"body\\\": \\\"## Summary\\nAdds login and register API endpoints.\\n\\nCloses #42\\\",\n    \\\"head\\\": \\\"$BRANCH\\\",\n    \\\"base\\\": \\\"main\\\"\n  }\"\n```\n\nThe response JSON includes the PR `number` — save it for later commands.\n\nTo create as a draft, add `\"draft\": true` to the JSON body.\n\n## 4. Monitoring CI Status\n\n### Check CI Status\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\n# One-shot check\ngh pr checks\n\n# Watch until all checks finish (polls every 10s)\ngh pr checks --watch\n```\n\n**With git + curl:**\n\n```bash\n# Get the latest commit SHA on the current branch\nSHA=$(git rev-parse HEAD)\n\n# Query the combined status\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/commits/$SHA/status \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\ndata = json.load(sys.stdin)\nprint(f\\\"Overall: {data['state']}\\\")\nfor s in data.get('statuses', []):\n    print(f\\\"  {s['context']}: {s['state']} - {s.get('description', '')}\\\")\"\n\n# Also check GitHub Actions check runs (separate endpoint)\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/commits/$SHA/check-runs \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\ndata = json.load(sys.stdin)\nfor cr in data.get('check_runs', []):\n    print(f\\\"  {cr['name']}: {cr['status']} / {cr['conclusion'] or 'pending'}\\\")\"\n```\n\n### Poll Until Complete (git + curl)\n\n```bash\n# Simple polling loop — check every 30 seconds, up to 10 minutes\nSHA=$(git rev-parse HEAD)\nfor i in $(seq 1 20); do\n  STATUS=$(curl -s \\\n    -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n    https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/commits/$SHA/status \\\n    | python3 -c \"import sys,json; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['state'])\")\n  echo \"Check $i: $STATUS\"\n  if [ \"$STATUS\" = \"success\" ] || [ \"$STATUS\" = \"failure\" ] || [ \"$STATUS\" = \"error\" ]; then\n    break\n  fi\n  sleep 30\ndone\n```\n\n## 5. Auto-Fixing CI Failures\n\nWhen CI fails, diagnose and fix. This loop works with either auth method.\n\n### Step 1: Get Failure Details\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\n# List recent workflow runs on this branch\ngh run list --branch $(git branch --show-current) --limit 5\n\n# View failed logs\ngh run view <RUN_ID> --log-failed\n```\n\n**With git + curl:**\n\n```bash\nBRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)\n\n# List workflow runs on this branch\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  \"https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/runs?branch=$BRANCH&per_page=5\" \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\nruns = json.load(sys.stdin)['workflow_runs']\nfor r in runs:\n    print(f\\\"Run {r['id']}: {r['name']} - {r['conclusion'] or r['status']}\\\")\"\n\n# Get failed job logs (download as zip, extract, read)\nRUN_ID=<run_id>\ncurl -s -L \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/runs/$RUN_ID/logs \\\n  -o /tmp/ci-logs.zip\ncd /tmp && unzip -o ci-logs.zip -d ci-logs && cat ci-logs/*.txt\n```\n\n### Step 2: Fix and Push\n\nAfter identifying the issue, use file tools (`patch`, `write_file`) to fix it:\n\n```bash\ngit add <fixed_files>\ngit commit -m \"fix: resolve CI failure in <check_name>\"\ngit push\n```\n\n### Step 3: Verify\n\nRe-check CI status using the commands from Section 4 above.\n\n### Auto-Fix Loop Pattern\n\nWhen asked to auto-fix CI, follow this loop:\n\n1. Check CI status → identify failures\n2. Read failure logs → understand the error\n3. Use `read_file` + `patch`/`write_file` → fix the code\n4. `git add . && git commit -m \"fix: ...\" && git push`\n5. Wait for CI → re-check status\n6. Repeat if still failing (up to 3 attempts, then ask the user)\n\n## 6. Merging\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\n# Squash merge + delete branch (cleanest for feature branches)\ngh pr merge --squash --delete-branch\n\n# Enable auto-merge (merges when all checks pass)\ngh pr merge --auto --squash --delete-branch\n```\n\n**With git + curl:**\n\n```bash\nPR_NUMBER=<number>\n\n# Merge the PR via API (squash)\ncurl -s -X PUT \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/pulls/$PR_NUMBER/merge \\\n  -d \"{\n    \\\"merge_method\\\": \\\"squash\\\",\n    \\\"commit_title\\\": \\\"feat: add user authentication (#$PR_NUMBER)\\\"\n  }\"\n\n# Delete the remote branch after merge\nBRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)\ngit push origin --delete $BRANCH\n\n# Switch back to main locally\ngit checkout main && git pull origin main\ngit branch -d $BRANCH\n```\n\nMerge methods: `\"merge\"` (merge commit), `\"squash\"`, `\"rebase\"`\n\n### Enable Auto-Merge (curl)\n\n```bash\n# Auto-merge requires the repo to have it enabled in settings.\n# This uses the GraphQL API since REST doesn't support auto-merge.\nPR_NODE_ID=$(curl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/pulls/$PR_NUMBER \\\n  | python3 -c \"import sys,json; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['node_id'])\")\n\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/graphql \\\n  -d \"{\\\"query\\\": \\\"mutation { enablePullRequestAutoMerge(input: {pullRequestId: \\\\\\\"$PR_NODE_ID\\\\\\\", mergeMethod: SQUASH}) { clientMutationId } }\\\"}\"\n```\n\n## 7. Complete Workflow Example\n\n```bash\n# 1. Start from clean main\ngit checkout main && git pull origin main\n\n# 2. Branch\ngit checkout -b fix/login-redirect-bug\n\n# 3. (Agent makes code changes with file tools)\n\n# 4. Commit\ngit add src/auth/login.py tests/test_login.py\ngit commit -m \"fix: correct redirect URL after login\n\nPreserves the ?next= parameter instead of always redirecting to /dashboard.\"\n\n# 5. Push\ngit push -u origin HEAD\n\n# 6. Create PR (picks gh or curl based on what's available)\n# ... (see Section 3)\n\n# 7. Monitor CI (see Section 4)\n\n# 8. Merge when green (see Section 6)\n```\n\n## Useful PR Commands Reference\n\n| Action | gh | git + curl |\n|--------|-----|-----------|\n| List my PRs | `gh pr list --author @me` | `curl -s -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \"https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/pulls?state=open\"` |\n| View PR diff | `gh pr diff` | `git diff main...HEAD` (local) or `curl -H \"Accept: application/vnd.github.diff\" ...` |\n| Add comment | `gh pr comment N --body \"...\"` | `curl -X POST .../issues/N/comments -d '{\"body\":\"...\"}'` |\n| Request review | `gh pr edit N --add-reviewer user` | `curl -X POST .../pulls/N/requested_reviewers -d '{\"reviewers\":[\"user\"]}'` |\n| Close PR | `gh pr close N` | `curl -X PATCH .../pulls/N -d '{\"state\":\"closed\"}'` |\n| Check out someone's PR | `gh pr checkout N` | `git fetch origin pull/N/head:pr-N && git checkout pr-N` |\n", "path": "github/github-pr-workflow/SKILL.md", "skill_dir": "/home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/skills/github/github-pr-workflow", "linked_files": {"references": ["references/ci-troubleshooting.md", "references/conventional-commits.md"], "templates": ["templates/pr-body-bugfix.md", "templates/pr-body-feature.md"]}, "usage_hint": "To view linked files, call skill_view(name, file_path) where file_path is e.g. 'references/api.md' or 'assets/config.yaml'", "required_environment_variables": [], "required_commands": [], "missing_required_environment_variables": [], "missing_credential_files": [], "missing_required_commands": [], "setup_needed": false, "setup_skipped": false, "readiness_status": "available", "metadata": {"hermes": {"tags": ["GitHub", "Pull-Requests", "CI/CD", "Git", "Automation", "Merge"], "related_skills": ["github-auth", "github-code-review"]}}}

{"success": true, "name": "file-write-verification", "description": "Verify every file write/patch against ground truth (git diff, ast.parse, py_compile, bash -n, git checkout to recover) — never trust the tool echo or lint status alone; repair pattern for mid-write content corruption; when to prefer patch-tool edits over full rewrites.", "tags": [], "related_skills": [], "content": "---\nname: file-write-verification\ndescription: \"Verify every file write/patch against ground truth (git diff, ast.parse, py_compile, bash -n, git checkout to recover) — never trust the tool echo or lint status alone; repair pattern for mid-write content corruption; when to prefer patch-tool edits over full rewrites.\"\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent\nlicense: MIT\n---\n\n# File-Write Verification Discipline\n\nClass-level rule for any session that writes or edits files via tool calls.\n\n## Why\n\nIn one session the write channel corrupted content mid-write 5+ times:\nphantom text injected into file contents and into tool-result echoes, writes\ntruncated mid-content, and one malformed call writing to a garbage path\n(`/home/jordatech/work adversely/...`). The tool's own success echo and lint\nstatus both reported \"ok\" on corrupted files. The corruption was invisible\nuntil verified against independent ground truth.\n\n## The rule\n\n**Never trust the write tool's echo or its lint verdict alone.** After every\nwrite/patch of a file that matters:\n\n1. **Verify syntax/structure independently:**\n   - Python: `python3 -m py_compile <f>` or `python3 -c \"import ast; ast.parse(open('<f>').read())\"`\n   - Shell: `bash -n <f>`\n   - YAML/TOML/JSON: parse it\n   - Expected size: sanity-check byte/line count against what you intended\n2. **Verify content against git:** `git diff <f>` is the authoritative record\n   of what actually landed. Review the diff before staging.\n3. **Never run repair loops against echo.** Re-read the file (read_file or\n   git diff) before any fix; assert pre-conditions before surgical edits.\n4. **Prefer patch-tool edits over full rewrites** for existing files — smaller\n   diff surface, independently verified by the fuzzy-match result.\n5. **Use git to recover:** `git checkout -- <f>` restores a known-good state\n   when a write mangles a tracked file; then re-apply changes as small patches.\n\n## Repair pattern (Python, for surgical fixes with pre-checks)\n\n```python\nfrom pathlib import Path\np = Path(\"path/to/file\")\nlines = p.read_text().splitlines()\n# ASSERT pre-conditions before touching anything — abort on surprise\nassert lines[74] == \"expected exact line\", lines[74]\ndel lines[179:181]  # drop duplicated fragment\np.write_text(\"\\n\".join(lines) + \"\\n\")\n```\n\n## sed -i with quoted replacements silently mangles quoting\n\nMulti-site `sed -i 's|…\\$(dirname \"\\$0\")…|…|'` edits on a deploy script produced\ncorrect-looking but broken quoting on 2 of 3 sites (a closing quote lost inside an\n`if \"$(dirname \"$0\")/healthcheck.sh $([ …` construct), while the third site looked\nright — caught only by printing the affected lines + `bash -n` (2026-09-27). sed's\nown exit code and the shell's silence are not evidence.\n\n- For shell scripts, prefer the patch tool (returns a reviewable unified diff) or a\n  python string-replace with exact-match assertions over `sed -i` when the pattern\n  contains quotes/brackets.\n- If sed is used anyway: immediately `sed -n '<affected>,<affected>p'` + `bash -n` —\n  never proceed on the sed exit code alone.\n\n## Red flags in tool results\n\n- Tool result text containing strings you never wrote (canary/injection\n  phrases, references to files you didn't touch) → treat the ENTIRE result as\n  suspect; verify on disk immediately.\n- A \"sibling subagent modified this file\" warning when you spawned no\n  subagents → usually the tracker reacting to your own unrecorded writes;\n  check git status/diff for ground truth.\n- Write succeeded to a path you didn't specify → `git status --porcelain`\n  for unexpected files, delete them, re-run git-clean checks.\n\n## No-lint file types (extra vigilance)\n\n`.html` (Jinja templates), `.md`, and other gate-less types get\n`lint: skipped` — syntax checks in step 1 are unavailable, so corruption\nsurvives until git diff or a render test. Observed corruption shapes in\nthese types (2026-09-26, two instances): stray closing tags inside `<p>`\n(`…stale guess.</h2></p>`), truncated table cells (`<td><a href=\"…\">{\">`),\nand — via the PATCH tool, not just write_file — a duplicated header line\ninserted mid-file in a markdown log. For these types ALWAYS: (a) review the\n`git diff` hunks line-by-line right after writing, and (b) when a test\nrenders the file, assert one string from every major section so truncation\nfails loudly.\n\n## When verification found corruption (repair order)\n\n1. `git checkout -- <file>` (if tracked and pre-write state is good)\n2. Re-apply intended change via patch tool (small, verifiable diffs)\n3. For untracked new files: python re-write with assertion pre-checks\n4. Re-verify: syntax check + git diff review + (for Python) import test\n\n## Non-durable failures (do NOT capture as rules)\n\nMissing binaries, path mismatches after migration, transient tool outages\nthat resolved in-session — these are environment state, not discipline. The\ndurable lesson is the verify-against-ground-truth pattern, not \"the tool is\nbroken\".", "path": "file-write-verification/SKILL.md", "skill_dir": "/home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/skills/file-write-verification", "linked_files": null, "usage_hint": null, "required_environment_variables": [], "required_commands": [], "missing_required_environment_variables": [], "missing_credential_files": [], "missing_required_commands": [], "setup_needed": false, "setup_skipped": false, "readiness_status": "available"}

{"success": true, "name": "proxmox-cluster-infrastructure", "description": "Build, configure, and verify Proxmox VE / Proxmox Datacenter Manager infrastructure: PVE API login, LXC provisioning, PDM setup, LLDAP access, remote cluster registration, no-subscription repo setup, and subscription-nag mitigation.", "tags": [], "related_skills": [], "content": "---\nname: proxmox-cluster-infrastructure\ndescription: \"Build, configure, and verify Proxmox VE / Proxmox Datacenter Manager infrastructure: PVE API login, LXC provisioning, PDM setup, LLDAP access, remote cluster registration, no-subscription repo setup, and subscription-nag mitigation.\"\n---\n\n# Proxmox Cluster Infrastructure\n\nUse this skill when asked to administer a Proxmox VE cluster, deploy Proxmox Datacenter Manager (PDM), add PVE nodes/remotes to PDM, configure LDAP/LLDAP access, or fix PDM post-install UI/repository issues.\n\n## Safety and boundaries\n\n- Treat Proxmox/PDM credentials, API tickets, CSRF tokens, generated API tokens, LDAP bind passwords, and root passwords as sensitive. Do **not** print them in final responses or save them in skills/memory.\n- Prefer API verification over GUI assumptions. If the GUI behavior is reported by the user, verify via API wherever possible, then state when a browser hard refresh/cache clear is still required.\n- When using community scripts, inspect the current raw script first and apply only the needed sections when running interactively is impractical.\n- For destructive operations (destroying CTs/VMs, replacing remotes, modifying ACLs), verify the target node/VMID/remote ID first and avoid affecting unrelated workloads.\n- **Node power boundaries (Jordan, 2026-09-11, explicit directive):** NEVER power off or restart `miam-00133` or `miam-00135` — they run critical services and the Hermes bot VMs (906 etc.). VM/guest restarts are fine; node restarts are not. `miam-00147` (and its ThunderBay via PDU 153 outlets 7/8) may be power-cycled for qualification work. Confirm live outlet labels in the PDU portal before every cycle.\n\n## Proxmox VE API login pattern\n\n1. Discover the authentication realm before assuming username format:\n   - `GET /api2/json/access/domains`\n   - Common working form for LLDAP-backed users is `username@LLDAP-Domain`.\n2. Authenticate:\n   - `POST /api2/json/access/ticket` with `username` and `password`.\n   - Store `PVEAuthCookie` in the session and send `CSRFPreventionToken` for POST/PUT/DELETE.\n3. Verify access with read-only endpoints:\n   - `GET /api2/json/version`\n   - `GET /api2/json/nodes`\n   - `GET /api2/json/access/permissions`\n4. Redact ticket and CSRF values in logs/summaries.\n\n### PVE API via LLDAP bot account — PRIMARY control path (proven 2026-09-10, user-directed)\n\nJordan set this as the primary access path (over pexpect/SSH): drive the PVE API with\nthe LLDAP bot account. Ready-made helper: `scripts/pve_api.py` (creds from a 600 file,\ntwo lines: username, password; default `~/.pve_ldap_bot`; cluster endpoint override via\n`PVE_HOST`).\n\n- Login: POST `/api2/json/access/ticket` with `username=<bot>@LLDAP-Domain` -> response\n  carries `ticket` + `CSRFPreventionToken`. Send cookie `PVEAuthCookie` on every call;\n  send `CSRFPreventionToken` header on non-GETs. `/access/domains` lists the realm\n  (`LLDAP-Domain`).\n- **Node short-names are MIXED across the cluster** — `miam00111/miam00112/miam00143/\n  miam00144` (no hyphen) vs `miam-00135` etc. (hyphen). Always enumerate\n  `GET /api2/json/nodes` and use the exact returned name; guessing yields\n  `500 hostname lookup failed`.\n- **Guest exec (the replacement for `qm guest exec`):** POST\n  `/nodes/<node>/qemu/<vmid>/agent/exec` with body\n  `{\"command\":[\"bash\",\"-lc\",\"echo <b64> | base64 -d | bash\"]}`, then poll\n  GET `/nodes/<node>/qemu/<vmid>/agent/exec-status?pid=<pid>` until `exited`; read\n  `exitcode` / `out-data` / `err-data`. TWO traps: (1) urlencode the body with\n  `doseq=True` — a plain urlencode silently DROPS list values, and the exec fails with\n  HTTP 596 / no pid, which looks like a broken guest agent but is a client bug;\n  (2) dispatch returns 500 when the VM is stopped — check vm status first.\n- Guest agent channel sanity probe: GET `/agent/get-osinfo` (cheap, works even when\n  exec routing misbehaves). VM stopped -> 500 with no data.\n- **PVE API parameter formats (2026-09-13, v0.11 session):** three distinct traps in one\n  endpoint family. (1) Array params are REPEATED plain keys, NOT `name[]=` — body\n  `command=bash&command=-lc&command=<script>`; `command[]=...` and JSON-string bodies both\n  400 with `Parameter verification failed`. (2) `agent/exec` does NOT accept a `timeout`\n  param (schema rejects it: `property is not defined in schema`) — dispatch returns a pid\n  and you enforce your own deadline while polling. (3) `/agent/exec` returns\n  `{\"data\":{\"pid\": N}}` (a DICT) — extracting `data['pid']` blindly throws; handle both\n  dict and bare-int shapes. (4) GET endpoints take params in the QUERYSTRING — passing\n  them as a POST-style body yields silent empty responses / 400s.\n- **LXC containers have NO `/lxc/<id>/exec` API endpoint on PVE 9.2** (POST returns\n  `501 Method 'POST /nodes/.../lxc/.../exec' not implemented`) — the qemu guest-agent exec\n  path is VM-only. For CT internals use SSH/node-shell (`pct exec`), or reach the CT's\n  services over the network from a VM that has the tooling (e.g. install\n  `postgresql-client` on the app VM and dump remotely instead of exec'ing into the DB CT).\n- **`pct exec <id> -- bash -c \"<script>\"` breaks on complex scripts** (2026-09-30 inventory\n  sweep): the script text crosses TWO shell layers (node SSH + pct exec), and `$`/backtick/quote\n  escaping that survives both is fragile — batched scans silently returned 59-byte empty outputs\n  with no error. Working pattern: base64 the script → write it to node `/tmp` → run it INSIDE\n  the CT from a file via stdin: `echo <b64> | base64 -d > /tmp/s_<id>.sh && pct exec <id> --\n  bash < /tmp/s_<id>.sh > /tmp/out_<id>.txt 2>&1; cat /tmp/out_<id>.txt`. Also: `pct exec` does\n  NOT reliably forward a piped stdin from the SSH command line — always go through a node-side file.\n- **Long guest commands through qga exec: run detached + result file.** qga exec (CLI or API)\n  blocks ~2 min and times out on sleeps. For \"wait N seconds then report\" probes, dispatch\n  `nohup bash -c '... > /tmp/result.txt 2>&1 &'` (returns \"started\" immediately), then poll with\n  a second exec that cats the result file.\n- **Root@pam ticket via `/access/ticket` works from a Python urllib client** when the\n  bash+curl path is unavailable (no jq / curl quirks) — same urlencoded form\n  (`username=root@pam`), same cookie/CSRF handling, self-signed TLS needs an\n  `ssl.CERT_NONE` context. Reusable pattern: stage creds in a 0600 two-line file, refresh\n  the ticket on 401 once, then proceed.\n- **`vmstatus` reads `/nodes/<node>/qemu/<vmid>/status/current`; `vmconfig` reads\n  `/nodes/<node>/qemu/<vmid>/config`. Both safe read-only.**\n- If cluster-forwarded API calls fail oddly (e.g. HTTP 596), retry against the node's\n  own endpoint `https://10.0.20.<node-digits>:8006` with the same bot ticket.\n\n### PDM API login differs from PVE (pitfall)\nProxmox Datacenter Manager is a SEPARATE service (not plain PVE); its auth is NOT the\nsame as a PVE cluster node login:\n- Endpoint is `https://<pdm-host>:8443/api2/json/access/ticket` (port 8443, not 8006).\n- Username form is `user@LLDAP-Domain` (same realm discovery trick).\n- The success payload keys are **`ticket-info` and `CSRFPreventionToken`** — there is NO\n  `ticket` key. Do not read `data['ticket']`. The `data` value may arrive as a\n  JSON-encoded STRING on some versions — parse defensively (`if isinstance(d, str):\n  d = json.loads(d)`), or a dict access throws `'str' object has no attribute 'get'`.\n- The auth cookie is **`__Host-PDMAuthCookie`** (a `__Host-` prefixed cookie), NOT\n  `PVEAuthCookie`. Persist it in a cookie jar (`curl -c/-b`) rather than hand-setting.\n- Passing the cookie to `GET /api2/json/remotes/remote` works (node list is enumerated in\n  the remote's `nodes` array as `ip,fingerprint=...`). Aggregate read endpoints like\n  `GET /api2/json/resources/list` are available; but fine-grained config (e.g. a VM's\n  `qm config`) is NOT proxied through PDM — reach the node's PVE host directly\n  (`/nodes/<node>/<resource>/<vmid>/config`) or SSH to the host for `qm config`.\n  - `resources/list` shape: `{\"data\":[{\"remote\":\"<remote-id>\",\"resources\":[...]}]}` — one\n    element per remote; entries are `pve-node` / `pve-storage` / `pve-qemu` / `pve-lxc`\n    with `vmid,node,status,name,tags`. Ideal for cluster-wide VMID/IP/name audits.\n  - Login also works as `jordatech@LLDAP-Domain`; use a curl `-c`/`-b` cookie jar (the\n    `__Host-PDMAuthCookie`) instead of hand-built Authorization headers.\n- `GET /api2/json/nodes` returns `permission check failed` for a limited LDAP account;\n  rely on `remotes/remote` + `resources/list` for node/guest enumeration instead.\n- If your PVE host layer needs full stack detail, SSH root to the host and run `qm`\n  directly — simpler and permission-complete than fighting PDM's API surface.\n\n### PVE API as PRIMARY access path (user directive 2026-09-11: \"Switch primary to the PVE API/web path\")\n\nUse the PVE REST API through an LLDAP bot account as the FIRST choice for cluster\noperations; password-SSH runners (noderun.py) drop to fallback for host-only tasks\nthe API can't reach.\n\n- **Bot creds**: staged as a 0600 file (`~/.pve_ldap_bot`: line 1 = username, line 2 =\n  password; realm suffix appended in code). Never echo, never commit.\n- **Auth**: `POST /api2/json/access/ticket` with urlencoded\n  `username=<user>@LLDAP-Domain&password=...` → then send cookie `PVEAuthCookie` on every\n  call + header `CSRFPreventionToken` for non-GET. TLS is self-signed → ssl context with\n  `check_hostname=False, verify_mode=CERT_NONE`.\n- **Ready-made helper**: `scripts/pve_api.py` (login / realms / nodes / resources /\n  permissions / vmconfig <node> <vmid> / vmstatus <node> <vmid> / raw <METHOD> <path> [body];\n  also importable: `from pve_api import api, load_creds`).\n- **Node short-names are INCONSISTENT** — ring nodes are `miam00111/miam00112/miam00143/\n  miam00144` (no hyphen) while `miam-00135`, `miam-00100`, `miam-00149` carry hyphens.\n  NEVER guess a node name in an API path; enumerate `GET /api2/json/nodes` first.\n- **Guest exec via API** (replaces qm guest exec + most SSH-to-VM work):\n  `POST /nodes/<node>/qemu/<vmid>/agent/exec` with body\n  `{\"command\": [\"bash\", \"-lc\", \"echo <b64-of-script> | base64 -d | bash\"]}`, then poll\n  `GET .../agent/exec-status?pid=<pid>` until `exited` (fields: exitcode, out-data,\n  err-data). **PITFALL: urlencode the body with `doseq=True`** — the `command` value is a\n  LIST, and plain urlencode silently DROPS list values, so the POST returns 200-ish while\n  the exec never runs (dispatch returns no pid / 596-looking failures).\n- **VM lifecycle** via `/nodes/<n>/qemu/<id>/status/start` (and stop/reboot) is clean and\n  audited by the bot identity; track completion via `/nodes/<n>/tasks/<upid>/status`.\n- **PDU power portal** (Jordan, 2026-09-11): web-managed PDU portal at\n  `https://10.0.20.154/` (nginx front; gunicorn binds 127.0.0.1:5000 — :5000 from outside\n  is refused; `http://` 301s to https). Creds staged in `~/.pdu_portal` (600).\n  **Login quirks:** the LLDAP username/password form REJECTED the staged root creds\n  (2026-09-11); the **emergency local administrator** form works — POST `/login` with\n  `mode=emergency`, `e_user`, `e_pass`. The post-login redirect Location is RELATIVE\n  (`/`) — resolve against the base URL or requests/urllib throws MissingSchema. The\n  first dashboard GET can exceed 10s; use a 30s timeout. Outlet rows carry `data-ip`,\n  `data-outlet`, and a label div (153/7 = MIAM-00147 NUC, 153/8 = MIAM-00148 ThunderBay)\n  — verify live labels before cycling. Control-plane only — power telemetry polling\n  stays on Emporia/GPU NVML per the standing rule (never poll PDU network endpoints\n  .151-.153 for telemetry).\n\n### PVE host/guest shell without sshpass (pexpect) + cloud-init & serial recipes\n\nWhen you must drive Proxmox hosts/VMs from a LAN Hermes box (no web GUI, no existing\ninjected key for the host, possibly no DHCP on the segment, optionally offline hosts),\nthe reusable recipes live in `references/pve-guest-vm-operations.md`, and a ready-made\ndrain-safe password-SSH script runner is `scripts/ssh_run.py` (runs a local .sh on a\nnode, saves output; reads the credential from the profile memory file at runtime and\nnever prints it). Key points:\n- pexpect (user-level `~/ssh-venv`) replaces missing `sshpass`; key-based variant for VMs.\n  Ready-made drain-loop runner: `scripts/ssh_run.py` (see scripts dir; handles the\n  empty/truncated `child.before` pitfall and reads the password from profile memory).\n  **Password auth REQUIRES pexpect** — `subprocess.run(..., input=PW)` fails silently\n  (Permission denied) because the password prompt appears AFTER stdin is consumed.\n  If `sshpass` is absent and uninstalled, port the loop to pexpect, never to input-feed.\n- ssh_run.py takes **IP addresses or full resolvable names, not bare node short-names**\n  (`miam00112` → \"Could not resolve hostname\"; use `10.0.20.112`).\n- **PITFALL: credential-extraction regexes coupling scripts to memory formatting.**\n  ssh_run-style scripts that parse the profile MEMORY.md for the password (`r\"root/(\\S+?)\\)\"`\n  etc.) break when memory entries are rewritten (a batch prune changed one entry's\n  phrasing and every helper died with `AssertionError: credential not found`). Make the\n  regex resilient (match `root/<word>` anywhere: `r\"root/(\\w+)\"`), or read the credential\n  from a dedicated file the scripts own — and after any memory rewrite, smoke-test one\n  helper before batch-driving the fleet.\n- **Never treat a `[Pp]assword` match after login as auth rejection** — `qm config`\n  prints a literal `cipassword: ****` line and VM login loops print `Password:`; check for\n  `Permission denied` instead, or you'll report false auth failures.\n- Host SSH rate-limits rapid reconnects → pace calls, batch commands into one base64'd\n  script run once.\n- **PITFALL: pexpect `child.before` empty/truncated on long batched output.** A single\n  `expect(EOF)` + one `child.before` read can return EMPTY (match at buffer start) or\n  lose most output. Drain instead: loop\n  `expect([r'[Pp]assword:', pexpect.EOF, pexpect.TIMEOUT])` until EOF, appending\n  `child.before` on every iteration, then `\"\".join()` the pieces. Keep the password\n  pattern inside the loop so late prompts still match; treat success as the absence of\n  `Permission denied` in the joined text.\n- Large binaries to offline hosts: serve via `python3 -m http.server` on the internet-\n  capable LAN host, `curl` inside the Proxmox host. pexpect-scp with `encoding='utf-8'`\n  corrupts binaries.\n\n### `qm guest exec` / `pct exec` / `pct push` only work on the OWNING node\n\n`/etc/pve` is cluster-readable, but guest-exec commands resolve the guest config against\nthe LOCAL node. Running `qm guest exec 103` from a node that doesn't own VM103 fails with\n`Configuration file 'nodes/<wrong-node>/qemu-server/103.conf' does not exist`. SSH to the\nowning node first (fleet map: VM103→miam-00111, VM401→miam-00112, VM109→miam-00143,\nVM111→miam-00144; CT115→miam-00147). `pct push` additionally requires the CT to be running.\n\n**PITFALL: `qm guest exec <vmid> -- bash -s` does NOT forward stdin.** A heredoc piped at\n`qm guest exec` feeds the node-side qm process, not the guest — the call returns\n`{\"exitcode\":0}` with EMPTY `out-data` while the guest script never ran. This silently\nno-ops entire batch installs. The reliable pattern is base64-in-command:\n`qm guest exec <vmid> -- bash -c \"echo <base64> | base64 -d | bash\"`.\nSame class of confusion: `scp node:/tmp/x` lands the file on the NODE — to move a file\nnode→CT use `pct push <ctid> /tmp/x /tmp/x` (and `pct pull` to fetch out).\n\n### Inspecting the GUEST vs the NODE (verify your vantage point)\n\nA script run directly through SSH lands on the NODE shell — its output (`pve-root` df,\nQEMU process list, host `lspci`, missing `nvidia-smi`) describes the hypervisor, NOT the\nguest. This burned a full diagnostic round: a \"bare guest\" verdict was drawn from node-shell\noutput while the guest was actually fully built (drivers + venv + model cache intact).\nRule: every guest-internal probe (cloud-init status, nvidia-smi, venv/model dirs, guest FS\nusage, guest internet) MUST be wrapped in `qm guest exec <vmid> -- bash -lc '...'` on the\nowning node. Before mutating anything based on an inspection, state which vantage point\nproduced the evidence.\n\n### Deploying a file INTO a VM guest when your box has no direct key\n\n1. base64 the deploy script locally; run it to the owning node over SSH, decode to /tmp there.\n2. From the node: `scp /tmp/x.sh <guest-user>@<guest-ip>:/tmp/` — the node holds the SSH\n   keypair baked into the guest's cloud-init `authorized_keys` (e.g. the `root@miam-00135`\n   RSA key referenced in cloud-config users).\n3. From the node: `ssh <guest-user>@<guest-ip> \"sudo bash /tmp/deploy.sh\"` — the cloud-init\n   user has NOPASSWD sudo. Direct SSH from your own box only works if YOUR key is in the\n   guest's authorized_keys; otherwise this node-relay path is the reliable route.\n\n### Debian 12 LXCs ship no sudo\n\n`sudo: command not found` inside a fresh Debian 12 CT. As root use\n`runuser -u <user> -- <cmd>` (e.g. `runuser -u postgres -- psql ...`). Locale/UTF-8 perl\nwarnings in these CTs are harmless noise.\n\n### Reconstructing an undocumented service into systemd from bash_history\n\nWhen a guest runs a critical service (e.g. vLLM) with no unit file, recover the final\nbattle-tested invocation from `grep -ihE '<service>' /root/.bash_history /home/*/.bash_history`\n—the LAST occurrence is the current working config. Turn it into\n`/etc/systemd/system/<svc>.service` (Environment lines for exports like HF_HOME, ExecStart\nwith all flags, Restart=on-failure, TimeoutStartSec generous for model load), then\n`daemon-reload && enable --now`, and verify via the service's HTTP endpoint\n(`/v1/models` + a small chat completion for vLLM) before calling it up.\n- Read a headless guest's console via `socat - UNIX-CONNECT:/var/run/qemu-server/<vmid>.serial0`\n  under `pty.fork()` — the only view before the guest agent/network are up. Verified\n  working pattern: `pty.fork()` + `os.execvp('socat', ...)`, write `\\n`, then\n  `select()`-loop reads for ~20s; a booting/idle guest yields the `login:` banner.\n  Wrap it in a script landed on the node via base64-heredoc, then `python3 <script>`\n  — nodes have no pexpect installed (stdlib `pty`/`select` only).\n- **cloud-init on the Ubuntu noble image applies network/creds ONLY on first boot.**\n  Bake `ipconfig0/ciuser/cipassword/sshkeys` BEFORE `qm start` on a freshly created VM.\n  Patching a booted VM then `qm reboot` does NOT re-apply them.\n  ### Re-run WITHOUT recreating: bump the cloud-init instance-id\n  When a first boot half-applied (ssh/packages missing, wrong IP) you do NOT need to\n  destroy/rebuild: edit the user snippet (`/var/lib/vz/snippets/<name>.yaml`), bump\n  `instance-id` in the meta snippet (e.g. `bootstrap-v2` → `v3`), set static IP via\n  `qm set <vmid> --ipconfig0 ip=<ip>/24,gw=<gw>`, then `qm stop <vmid> && qm start <vmid>`\n  (start regenerates the cloud-init ISO; the instance-id change forces cloud-init to\n  re-run per-instance modules). Poll readiness with `timeout 2 bash -c 'echo > /dev/tcp/<ip>/22'`\n  + `qm agent <vmid> ping`, then verify `cloud-init status --long` = done/no errors.\n  ### Static vs DHCP on vmbr0: VERIFY befo

# AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous agents working in ACMS.

## Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, define mandatory governance boundaries, and approve merges.

Agents may inspect the repository, plan, implement approved requirements, decompose approved scope into tasks, create tests, update affected documentation, create handoffs, add out-of-scope discoveries to `FUTURE_WORK.md`, draft ADRs/TDRs, and execute non-destructive work inside explicitly approved scope/budget/security/environment boundaries.

Agents must not silently add/remove product scope, promote future work, accept their own new significant ADR, merge their own feature work, exceed a budget, cross tenant/context boundaries, deploy to production, alter credentials/permissions, perform destructive infrastructure actions, or modify protected Organization/Product/Project/Feature context without required human authorization.

## Read before planning

For every meaningful change read:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/BUSINESS_PROBLEMS.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. relevant accepted/proposed ADRs
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope/follow-up work is involved
8. `INITIAL_IDEAS.md` when source provenance matters

## Requirement discipline

Implementation must trace to one or more `ACMS-REQ-###` entries. Before editing summarize the requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, files/components likely to change, assumptions, and validation.

If work is outside approved requirements, ask for human approval or record it in `FUTURE_WORK.md`.

## Future-work provenance

Every future-work item must identify one of:

- **Human-Directed**
- **Agent-Discovered**
- **Source-Derived**

Human-Directed does not mean approved; it remains future scope until promoted.

## Centralized governance, decentralized execution

ACMS controls approved assignment, scope, budget, context access, permissions, and governance. It must not become the synchronous permission service for every shell command/tool call/test/retry.

A worker agent may autonomously execute implementation details inside its approved envelope.

An agent may have:

- one primary ACMS work assignment; and
- zero or more registered background routines/cron jobs.

## Context authority

Protected context levels:

- Organization
- Product
- Project
- Feature

Agents may propose changes but may not modify these without human authorization or explicit delegated authority.

Agent, Task, and Live Session context may be modified within approved policy.

If approved scope appears wrong, execute it as far as reasonably possible, preserve state, create a handoff, and propose the alternate direction instead of silently changing scope.

## Handoffs

Meaningful work must produce/update a Markdown handoff recording:

1. assigned goal and requirement IDs;
2. what was attempted;
3. what actually changed;
4. what the agent believes the current state is;
5. validation and results;
6. blockers/assumptions/unresolved questions;
7. recommended next action.

## ADR policy

Human-explicit durable architecture decisions may be recorded as `Accepted`. Agent-originated significant decisions remain `Proposed` until human approval.

## Approval timeout policy

- Reversible in-scope decision: checkpoint/preserve state, use documented fallback, continue if safe.
- Consequential but deferable action: defer that action and continue unrelated work.
- Mandatory human gate: never infer approval from timeout; hold/deny the gated action and continue only unrelated authorized work.

## Security

- Never commit credentials/secrets/customer-sensitive data.
- External/customer agents are a separate trust domain.
- Internal Agent Bridge endpoints are private-network/VPN endpoints unless an accepted ADR says otherwise.
- Do not weaken authentication/authorization/audit to simplify implementation.

## Git workflow

The documentation bootstrap is human-authorized for direct import. After bootstrap, feature work must use a dedicated branch and PR.

Recommended branch: `type/ACMS-short-description`.

Recommended commit: `type(scope): short description (ACMS-REQ-###)` plus issue/ticket when available.

Every feature PR must list requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, validation, risks, ADR/TDR changes, future work, and reviewer focus. Agents do not self-merge.

{"output": "#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"PVE API helper for the MARION homelab - authenticates via the LLDAP bot\naccount (creds staged in a 0600 file: line 1 username, line 2 password).\nNever prints credentials. Verified working 2026-09-11 for login, node/guest\nenumeration, vm config/status, and guest-agent exec dispatch/polling.\n\nUsage:\n  pve_api.py login\n  pve_api.py realms|nodes|resources|permissions\n  pve_api.py vmconfig <node> <vmid>\n  pve_api.py vmstatus <node> <vmid>\n  pve_api.py raw <METHOD> <path> [body-string]\n\nAlso importable for scripts:\n  from pve_api import api, load_creds\n  status, data = api(\"POST\", \"/api2/json/nodes/<node>/qemu/<vmid>/agent/exec\",\n                     body={\"command\": [\"bash\", \"-lc\", \"echo <b64> | base64 -d | bash\"]})\n\"\"\"\nimport sys, json, ssl, urllib.request, urllib.parse, os\n\nBASE = \"https://10.0.20.100:8006\"\nCTX = ssl.create_default_context()\nCTX.check_hostname = False\nCTX.verify_mode = ssl.CERT_NONE\nCREDS = os.path.expanduser(\"~/.pve_ldap_bot\")\n\n\ndef load_creds():\n    with open(CREDS) as f:\n        lines = [l.rstrip(\"\\n\") for l in f if l.strip()]\n    return lines[0], lines[1]\n\n\ndef login():\n    user, pw = load_creds()\n    data = urllib.parse.urlencode({\"username\": user + \"@LLDAP-Domain\", \"password\": pw}).encode()\n    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + \"/api2/json/access/ticket\", data=data)\n    with urllib.request.urlopen(req, context=CTX, timeout=10) as r:\n        d = json.load(r)[\"data\"]\n    return d[\"ticket\"], d[\"CSRFPreventionToken\"], user + \"@LLDAP-Domain\"\n\n\ndef api(method, path, body=None):\n    ticket, csrf, _ = login()\n    return raw_req(method, path, ticket, csrf, body)\n\n\ndef raw_req(method, path, ticket, csrf, body=None):\n    data = None\n    if isinstance(body, dict):\n        # doseq=True is CRITICAL: list-valued params (e.g. agent/exec \"command\"\n        # array) are silently DROPPED by plain urlencode -> the POST succeeds\n        # but the exec never runs.\n        data = urllib.parse.urlencode(body, doseq=True).encode()\n    elif isinstance(body, str):\n        data = body.encode()\n    r = urllib.request.Request(BASE + path, data=data, method=method)\n    if ticket:\n        r.add_header(\"Cookie\", \"PVEAuthCookie=\" + ticket)\n    if csrf and method not in (\"GET\",):\n00-BASELINE-20261001.md\n2026-10-01-ACMS-A2A-PRODUCTION-PATH-FINAL-HANDOFF.md\nCHECKPOINT-1.md\nCHECKPOINT-2.md\nCHECKPOINT-3.md\nHANDOFF-20261001-ACMS-A2A-PRODUCTION-PATH.md\nPLAN-OF-RECORD.md\nROLLBACK.md\nSESSION-STATE.md\nSTEER-LOG.md\nVM123-DISPOSITION.md", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}
