{"success": true, "name": "acms-project-operations", "file": "references/worker-fleet-provisioning-2026-10-01.md", "content": "# Worker fleet provisioning from golden template 121 — 2026-10-01 session\n\nMulti-worker clone → re-identify → bootstrap → register recipe as executed for\nacms-worker-002..005 (STEA-004 A2A production path, Phase 6). Extends\n`references/hermes-worker-bringup.md` (single-worker bring-up on VM124) with the\nmulti-clone path and the traps that only show up at fleet scale. Facts below are\nlimited to what the session verified via live tool output (PVE API, qga exec,\nHTTP probes).\n\n## Placement policy (Jordan, 2026-10-01 — authoritative)\n\n- **acms-worker-001 (VM124) STAYS on miam00111.** CPUs differ across MARION\n  nodes; `cpu=host` VMs must never live- or cold-migrate across CPU-different\n  nodes. One worker per inference node is explicitly OK.\n- New workers: 002→miam00112 (VM125), 003→miam00143 (VM126), 004→miam00144\n  (VM127), 005→miam-00100 (VM128 — primary test worker, cloud+local inference).\n- MIAM00100 = default-preferred host for NEW general AI-agent workers;\n  MIAM00133/MIAM00147 excluded (critical services/backup).\n- MIAM-00112 has a known failing DIMM (channel#7, ECC errors persist across\n  reboot) — Jordan chose to proceed for modest worker load; on record for\n  hardware service.\n- **Node-name format trap:** PVE API uses `miam00111`, `miam00112`,\n  `miam00143`, `miam00144` (no dash) but `miam-00100` (dash). Always list nodes\n  from `/api2/json/nodes` first — name-format assumptions 500.\n\n## CRITICAL: template 121 bakes a STATIC netplan (.203) and NO Hermes runtime\n\n- PVE config shows `ipconfig0: ip=dhcp`, but that is cosmetic — cloud-init is\n  disabled in the image (`/etc/cloud/cloud-init.disabled`) and `/etc/netplan`\n  pins `10.0.20.203/24` (worker-001's live IP).\n- **Every clone of 121 boots into an instant IP collision with the live\n  worker-001.** Observed 2026-10-01: four fresh clones all claimed .203 and ARP\n  resolved .203 to one of the CLONES' MACs (bc:24:11:10:80:fe = VM127) — the\n  exact impostor-IP failure class of the 2026-09-29 VM108 case, at 4× scale.\n- Template also carries NO Hermes runtime: no `/home/hermes`, no\n  `/opt/hermes-venv`, no systemd units — only legacy `st-agentd` (port 8765,\n  ignored by the ACMS path). VM124's Hermes was installed manually 2026-09-27\n  via the pip bootstrap (see hermes-worker-bringup.md).\n- Template hostname lineage (`miam00111-vllm-vm103`) is baked in too.\n\n## PVE provisioning sequence (offline = cross-CPU-safe)\n\n1. **Clone:** `POST /nodes/{src}/qemu/121/clone {newid, full: 1, target,\n   storage: testthin, format: raw}` — 4 clones in parallel OK (~2.5 min total).\n2. **Offline storage-migrate while STOPPED:** `POST\n   /nodes/{src}/qemu/{id}/migrate {target, targetstorage: local-lvm}` — pass\n   `targetstorage` when the source storage (testthin) is absent/empty on the\n   destination. A 200G thin clone copies allocated blocks only (~5–15 min).\n   `cpu=host` is safe here because the VM boots on the DESTINATION's CPU.\n   The qmigrate preflight is the real CPU/compat check — there is no\n   reliable cpu-models endpoint (501 on this PVE build).\n3. **Start:** `POST /nodes/{dst}/qemu/{id}/status/start`. First boot ~60–90 s;\n   `agent/get-osinfo` answering = qga ready.\n4. **Task polling correctness:** check\n   `/nodes/{src}/tasks/{upid}/status` until `status == \"stopped\"`;\n   `exitstatus == \"OK\"` means success. A naive poller that treats\n   `stopped` as failure misreads every finished task (hit live).\n\n## Clone re-identification recipe (run on EVERY clone after first boot)\n\n```bash\nsystemctl stop hermes-bridge 2>/dev/null || true   # not present on fresh clones\nrm -f /etc/machine-id /var/lib/dbus/machine-id && systemd-machine-id-setup\nrm -f /etc/ssh/ssh_host_* && dpkg-reconfigure -f noninteractive openssh-server  # or: ssh-keygen -A\nhostnamectl set-hostname <worker-name>\n# netplan rewrite (below), chmod 600, netplan apply\n```\n\n- `systemd-machine-id-setup` stderr \"Initializing machine ID from VM UUID\" is\n  normal (derives from the per-VM UUID, so clones still diverge).\n- **Free-IP verification BEFORE assigning:** ping sweep from a LAN host (ssh\n  root@10.0.20.122 works; the workstation's ICMP is firewalled and will report\n  false \"free\") + `ip neigh` ARP on that host — STALE `bc:24:11:*` entries mean\n  a Proxmox VM holds the IP; match ARP MACs against each clone's real `net0`\n  MAC from PVE config. Assigned 2026-10-01: .206/.207/.208/.209 (verified free).\n\n## Hermes bootstrap per clone (proven pattern)\n\nStage `/tmp/bootstrap.sh` via qga base64, run under **nohup** (qga wedges on\nlong execs), log `/tmp/worker-bootstrap.log`:\n\n```bash\nid hermes 2>/dev/null || useradd -m -s /bin/bash hermes\npython3 -m venv /opt/hermes-venv\n/opt/hermes-venv/bin/pip install --quiet --upgrade pip\n/opt/hermes-venv/bin/pip install --quiet hermes-agent\n```\n\n~2 min/VM when all four run in parallel. Verify with\n`/opt/hermes-venv/bin/hermes --version` (0.19.0 on 2026-10-01).\n\n## Per-worker Hermes config (after bootstrap — the still-pending part on the clones)\n\n- **Agent naming convention (Jordan directive, 2026-10-01):**\n  `acms-hermes-worker-uid-###` — harness prefix (`hermes` today, e.g.\n  `acms-codex-worker-uid-###` for a different harness later); **the UID is THE\n  unique identifier** used to distinguish agents regardless of harness.\n- Profile: `~/.hermes/profiles/<name>/config.yaml` — custom_providers[0]\n  (`llm-manager` → `http://10.0.20.108:8080/v1`, plain-LAN nginx surface; the\n  :443 self-signed TLS breaks OpenAI clients) with\n  `models: [fast, qwen3.6-35b-a3b, code, gemma4-26b-a4b, qwen3.8-flash-next]`;\n  `model.default: qwen3.8-flash-next`; `context_length: 70000`,\n  `max_tokens: 8192`, `tools.tool_search.enabled: \"on\"`.\n- **Changing a worker's effective model live** (done on VM124 this session):\n  edit the profile config (append alias to custom_providers.models + set\n  model.default), keep a `.bak-pre-*` copy, `systemctl restart hermes-bridge`.\n  Unmatched request-model aliases are silently ignored (model_routes caveat) —\n  the worker config owns the mapping.\n- Systemd unit (created manually on VM124, template has none):\n  `hermes-bridge.service` — User=hermes,\n  `EnvironmentFile=/etc/llm-manager-agent.env` (LLM_MANAGER_AGENT_KEY +\n  API_SERVER_KEY), `ExecStart=/opt/hermes-venv/bin/hermes gateway run --profile\n  <name> --replace`. Gateway run takes NO --host/--port — those live in the\n  profile under `platforms.api_server.extra.{host,port,key}` (0.0.0.0:8402).\n  A drop-in alone does NOT change the ExecStart profile arg.\n- Each worker needs its OWN LLM Manager virtual key (per-agent keys are the\n  auth layer; VM124 uses `agent_key_acms_worker_001` 0600 on VM114). Issue\n  siblings via SM `POST /api/service/agents/provision` (idempotent) or LiteLLM\n  `/key/generate`.\n- Verify: plain `GET :8402/health` → `{\"status\":\"ok\",\"version\":\"0.19.0\"}` +\n  `/health/detailed` with bearer API_SERVER_KEY (readiness: state_db / config /\n  model / gateway all ok).\n\n## ACMS registration + bridge wiring (per new worker)\n\n- **Register:** `POST https://10.0.20.122/api/v1/agents/register` (NOT\n  `/api/v1/agents` — that 405s) with `{external_registration_id: \"arm-<name>\",\n  display_name: <final name>, trust_class: \"internal\", harness: \"hermes\",\n  bridge_version: \"0.19.0\", protocol_version: \"1\"}` → 201 + agent_id. Bearer =\n  ACMS_ADMIN_TOKEN from /opt/acms/.env (never echo it).\n- **Set the final display name AT REGISTRATION — there is NO display-name\n  PATCH endpoint** (404 on `/api/v1/agents/{id}`, hit live). The 4 agents were\n  registered as `acms-worker-00N` before the naming directive arrived; they\n  need re-registration or a new rename endpoint.\n- **Bridge resolution** (`acms/bridge_discovery.py`): SM\n  `/api/v1/agent-runtimes` (bearer ACMS_SERVER_MANAGER_TOKEN on CT122) carries\n  identity (acms_agent_id ↔ runtime_id, node/vmid, state_sync_health) but\n  `bridge_base_url` is NULL → the manual fallback `ACMS_BRIDGE_TARGETS_JSON`\n  supplies base_url + api_key. New workers = append entries to that JSON in\n  `/opt/acms/.env` + **container RECREATE** (compose env resolves at create\n  time; a restart is NOT enough — the credential-activation trap).\n- Model policy: migration 0012 seeds the system default\n  (local-preferred / qwen3.8-flash-next / cloud allowed); per-agent or\n  per-work-item overrides are optional rows in `model_policies`.\n\n## State: FLEET COMPLETE (2026-10-01, second session)\n\n- ALL PENDING ITEMS DONE: profiles + bridge units + env files on VM125-128;\n  per-worker LLM Manager keys `agent_key_acms_worker_002..005` (0600 on VM114,\n  each proven against LiteLLM); ARM runtime records via direct DB insert\n  (adopted-VM semantics w/ honest ownership_meta — no adopt API exists);\n  ACMS_BRIDGE_TARGETS_JSON 1→5 entries + container RECREATE (pinned image).\n- **aiohttp TRAP (hit on every clone):** the pip bootstrap installs hermes-agent\n  ONLY; the api_server platform needs `pip install aiohttp` + bridge restart or\n  the gateway logs \"No adapter available for api_server\" and :8402 never\n  listens while systemd shows active. Add to the pre-clone checklist.\n- Naming RESOLVED: ACMS PR #60 added migration 0013 (`legacy_name` + `worker_uid`\n  columns, backfill legacy_name=display_name) + admin-gated\n  `PATCH /api/v1/agents/{agent_id}` (display-only; UUID/external id IMMUTABLE).\n  All 5 renamed live to `acms-hermes-worker-uid-00N`; UUIDs preserved.\n- Final handoff: `~/acms-a2a-20261001/2026-10-01-ACMS-A2A-PRODUCTION-PATH-FINAL-HANDOFF.md`\n  (+ PLAN-OF-RECORD/STEER-LOG/CHECKPOINT-1..3/ROLLBACK/VM123-DISPOSITION).\n- VM123 (stopped duplicate acms-worker-001, miam00111): disposition documented,\n  NOT destroyed — holds static .203 netplan; NEVER start while VM124 owns .203.\n", "file_type": ".md"}

{"content": "1|#!/usr/bin/env python3\n2|\"\"\"Drain-hardened password-SSH runner for Proxmox MIAM nodes (pexpect).\n3|\n4|Successor to scripts/ssh_run.py: fixes the empty-output failure mode where a single\n5|expect(EOF) + child.before read returned truncated/empty output. This runner:\n6|  1. spawns ssh, waits for the [Pp]assword: prompt, sends the credential\n7|  2. feeds the script as base64 (no quoting/PTY-mangling issues for the script TEXT)\n8|  3. loops expect([password, END-marker, EOF, TIMEOUT]), appending child.before on\n9|     EVERY iteration until the END marker or EOF\n10|  4. flags auth failure only on a SECOND password prompt or \"Permission denied\"\n11|     (never on a literal `cipassword: ****` line from qm config output)\n12|\n13|Reads the credential from /home/jordatech/.miam_root_pass (root password shared by the\n14|MIAM Proxmox nodes AND the OPNsense web UI). Never prints it.\n15|\n16|Usage (module):   from noderun import run; run(\"10.0.20.135\", script_text, timeout=120)\n17|Usage (CLI):      python3 noderun.py <host> <script-file.sh>\n18|\"\"\"\n19|import base64\n20|import sys\n21|import time\n22|\n23|import pexpect\n24|\n25|END = \"___RUN_DONE_42___\"\n26|\n27|\n28|def run(host: str, script: str, user: str = \"root\", timeout: int = 120) -> str:\n29|    pw = open(\"/home/jordatech/.miam_root_pass\").read().strip()\n30|    b64 = base64.b64encode(script.encode()).decode()\n31|    wrapped = f\"echo {b64} | base64 -d | bash; echo {END}\"\n32|    child = pexpect.spawn(\n33|        \"/usr/bin/ssh\",\n34|        [\"-o\", \"StrictHostKeyChecking=no\", \"-o\", \"UserKnownHostsFile=/dev/null\",\n35|         \"-o\", \"ConnectTimeout=10\", \"-l\", user, host, wrapped],\n36|        encoding=\"utf-8\", timeout=timeout, maxread=65536)\n37|    pieces = []\n38|    sent = False\n39|    deadline = time.time() + timeout\n40|    while time.time() < deadline:\n41|        i = child.expect([r\"[Pp]assword\\s*:\", END, pexpect.EOF, pexpect.TIMEOUT],\n42|                         timeout=15)\n43|        if i == 0 and not sent:\n44|            child.sendline(pw)\n45|            sent = True\n46|        elif i == 0 and sent:\n47|            # a second password prompt after sending = auth failure\n48|            pieces.append(child.before or \"\")\n49|            break\n50|        elif i == 1:\n51|            pieces.append(child.before or \"\")\n52|            break\n53|        elif i == 2:\n54|            pieces.append(child.before or \"\")\n55|            break\n56|        else:\n57|            pieces.append(child.before or \"\")\n58|    child.close()\n59|    text = \"\".join(pieces)\n60|    text = text.replace(\"\\r\\n\", \"\\n\").replace(\"\\r\", \"\\n\")\n61|    if \"Permission denied\" in text:\n62|        text += \"\\n*** AUTH FAILED ***\"\n63|    return text\n64|\n65|\n66|if __name__ == \"__main__\":\n67|    host = sys.argv[1]\n68|    path = sys.argv[2]\n69|    script = open(path).read()\n70|    print(run(host, script))\n71|", "total_lines": 70, "file_size": 2628, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

{"success": true, "name": "proxmox-cluster-infrastructure", "file": "references/opnsense-kea-dhcp.md", "content": "# OPNsense Kea DHCP management via scripted MVC API (leases + static reservations)\n\nSession-verified recipe (2026-09-10) for reading DHCP state and managing static\nreservations on the MARION-lab OPNsense firewall. Extends `opnsense-unbound-dns.md` —\nread that first for the auth chain (UI session login with hidden-CSRF replay +\n`login=1`, then X-CSRFToken regexed from any `/ui/...` page's `$.ajaxSetup` JS).\n\n## Access facts\n\n- **UI credential: username `root` + the Proxmox node root password** (the same\n  `~/.miam_root_pass` used for the nodes) — verified working 2026-09-10. LDAP password\n  and username `admin` both fail.\n- **Vantage point matters**: DHCP-pool guests (e.g. CT906 @ 10.0.20.19x) CANNOT reach\n  `10.0.10.1:80` (timeout). A cluster node CAN (routes via `default via 10.0.20.1`).\n  Run the session script on a Proxmox node, not from pool-range guests. Ports: 80 open,\n  22 closed.\n- One CSRF token from any /ui page authorizes all MVC API calls in the session.\n\n## Kea endpoint map (discover module endpoints from the /ui page JS)\n\nOPNsense MVC modules expose grid endpoints as `/api/<module>/<verb>_<grid_id>/`\n(composed in page JS as `search: '/api/kea/dhcpv4/search_' + grid_id` etc.). To find\nthem: fetch the module's /ui page and regex `/api/<module>/[a-zA-Z0-9/_-]+` out of the\nsource. Verified endpoints:\n\n- **Active leases**: `GET /api/kea/leases4/search/` →\n  `{\"total\":N,\"rows\":[{\"address\",\"hwaddr\",\"hostname\",\"state\"}]}`\n- **Static reservations** (grid id `reservation`):\n  - list: `GET /api/kea/dhcpv4/search_reservation/` → rows: `uuid, subnet (uuid),\n    %subnet, ip_address, hw_address, hostname, description`\n  - add: `POST /api/kea/dhcpv4/add_reservation/` body\n    `{\"reservation\": {\"subnet\": \"<subnet-uuid>\", \"ip_address\": \"10.0.20.x\",\n    \"hw_address\": \"bc:24:11:xx\", \"hostname\": \"name\", \"description\": \"...\"}}`\n    → `{\"result\":\"saved\",\"uuid\":...}`. Subnet uuid comes from an existing row.\n  - CSV export: `GET /api/kea/dhcpv4/download_reservations` (semicolon-CSV, NOT JSON)\n  - CSV import: `POST /api/kea/dhcpv4/upload_reservations` (multipart — returned\n    `{\"status\":\"failed\"}` on first attempt; prefer the JSON add endpoint)\n  - apply: `POST /api/kea/service/reconfigure` `{}` — **may return `{\"status\":\"failed\"}`\n    even when the reservation persisted**; verify via `search_reservation/` and\n    `/api/kea/service/status` (`\"running\"`) instead of trusting the reconfigure result.\n- General model: `GET /api/kea/dhcpv4/get` → `{\"dhcpv4\": {\"general\", \"lexpire\", \"ha\"}}`\n  (subnet/reservation detail is NOT here — use the grid endpoints).\n\n## Static-IP assignment procedure (used for the Hermes pilot CT)\n\n1. Enumerate claimed addresses IN THIS ORDER:\n   a. `/etc/pve/corosync.conf` `ring0_addr` on any node — the DEFINITIVE bare-metal\n      node map (includes powered-off nodes; a ping sweep missed offline MIAM-00110 @\n      10.0.20.110 until Jordan corrected: \"10.0.20.110 is definitely used\").\n   b. Kea leases (pool .190–.250) + Kea reservations (statics: .151-.153 PDUs, .207\n      llm-manager, .187 hermes-pilot).\n   c. Live ARP (`ip neigh` — only entries WITH an lladdr are claimants; a `FAILED`\n      entry with no MAC is your own probe's negative cache, not a conflict) + ping/arping.\n2. Pick the lowest free address OUTSIDE the DHCP pool; set the guest static (netplan for\n   Ubuntu CTs; keep nameservers = 10.0.20.1).\n3. **Add a Kea reservation for the guest MAC in the same pass** so DHCP can never lease\n   the address to anyone else (`add_reservation` + verify + service check).\n4. Re-verify the workload E2E from the new IP before reporting done (e.g. chat\n   completion through the gateway).\n\nGuest MAC for an LXC: `pct exec <ctid> -- ip link show eth0` (or read from the Kea\nlease it currently holds).\n", "file_type": ".md"}

[Subdirectory context discovered: work/llm-manager-project-framework/AGENTS.md]
# AGENTS.md — LLM Manager

These instructions apply to AI agents modifying the LLM Manager repository or MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager deployment.

## 1. Read before changing anything

Read, in order:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `FUTURE_WORK.md`
4. `SPRINT.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md`
7. `docs/OPERATIONS_AND_USAGE.md`
8. relevant ADRs/TDRs

Approved requirements define scope. Future work is not authorization to implement.

## 2. Service boundary

LLM Manager owns inference/runtime infrastructure concerns. ACMS owns work/project/context orchestration.

Do not move project requirements, Kanban/task state, persistent transcript history, human steering governance, or general ACMS context management into LLM Manager merely because an integration needs them.

## 3. Production safety rules

- Treat MARION-IA-USA as production infrastructure.
- Never replace a known-working model/runtime before capturing its exact rollback configuration.
- Put deployments into explicit maintenance before authorized manual work.
- Recovery has historically had more than one trigger; verify both desired service state and desired power/VM state before stopping a VM.
- Do not assume a stopped model stays stopped until recovery state is verified.
- Never start two VMs that share the same passthrough GPU devices.
- Avoid repeated rapid GPU-VM stop/start cycles on MIAM-00111; a known PCIe/AER/riser condition has previously required a host reboot to recover.
- Preserve custom/patched vLLM environments. Do not `pip install -U vllm` into a known-good runtime.
- Experimental model stacks belong in versioned paths/containers/VMs.

## 4. Configuration and model truth

The live registry and verified runtime state are authoritative for current deployments.

Do not infer that an alias is local merely from its name. Verify provider/runtime/model identity.

When documenting a model deployment, capture:

- actual artifact/revision/checksum;
- runtime/image/commit;
- quantization;
- host/VM/site;
- context;
- TP/PP/DP/EP and relevant runtime flags;
- RAM/VRAM/hardware;
- route/alias;
- health and benchmark evidence.

## 5. History and rollback

Deployment history must make engineering work reproducible.

- `Load Configuration` is non-destructive.
- `Restore & Serve` is a separate confirmed operation.
- Failed experiments may be retained but hidden by default.
- Do not delete the last known-good revision merely because a newer model works.

## 6. Benchmark discipline

A performance number without deployment context is misleading.

Link benchmarks to exact deployment revisions and record at least:

- TTFT;
- output tokens/sec per request/agent;
- aggregate output tokens/sec;
- concurrency;
- prompt/output token counts;
- tested context;
- RAM/VRAM;
- host/hardware/runtime;
- errors;
- representative Hermes/tool-use status when relevant.

When comparing models, prefer same-node tests. When comparing nodes, use the same model/configuration first so hardware effects are visible.

## 7. Secrets and data

Never commit:

- `.env` files containing secrets;
- API keys/tokens;
- passwords;
- SSH/private keys;
- production database row dumps;
- customer-sensitive data;
- raw logs containing credentials.

Sanitize configuration into `.example` files. Run a secret scan before commit. If a secret appears in a tool transcript or repository candidate, stop and surface it for rotation/removal.

## 8. Git workflow

- Work on a dedicated branch.
- Branch format: `type/ticket-short-description`.
- Commit format: `type(scope): short description (#issue-id)`.
- Do not invent an issue ID; create/use an approved issue according to repository policy.
- Keep capture/bootstrapping changes reviewable.
- A human must approve before merge.

## 9. ADR policy

If a human explicitly made a significant architecture decision, record it as an ADR and surface it in the PR.

If the agent thinks a significant new decision is required, draft `Status: Proposed` and stop for human approval before treating it as accepted.

## 10. TDR policy

Use a TDR for intentional shortcuts, patched runtimes, manual recovery procedures, missing automation, known hardware/software limitations, or other accepted debt.

## 11. Validation

Run the exact project validation commands once captured/documented. At minimum for a Python service, consider syntax/import checks, tests, config parsing, migration/schema verification, and API smoke tests in a safe environment.

Report `Pass`, `Fail`, or `Not run` accurately. Never infer a passing test.

## 12. Stop and ask for human judgment when

- requirements conflict;
- an ACMS-facing contract would be invented or materially changed;
- a destructive production action is required outside the approved maintenance scope;
- credentials/access are required and unavailable;
- a security boundary changes;
- production tests fail unexpectedly;
- a new persistent model policy would be made without approved authority;
- the diff expands beyond the approved capture/documentation scope.

## 13. Handoff standard

At completion provide:

1. requirements addressed;
2. source/code captured and source locations;
3. files excluded and why;
4. changes made;
5. validation commands/results;
6. production impact (if any);
7. ADR/TDR status;
8. future-work items discovered;
9. known risks/unresolved questions;
10. exact branch, commit, PR, and recommended next action.

## 3. Production safety rules

- Treat MARION-IA-USA as production infrastructure.
- Never replace a known-working model/runtime before capturing its exact rollback configuration.
- Put deployments into explicit maintenance before authorized manual work.
- Recovery has historically had more than one trigger; verify both desired service state and desired power/VM state before stopping a VM.
- Do not assume a stopped model stays stopped until recovery state is verified.
- Never start two VMs that share the same passthrough GPU devices.
- Avoid repeated rapid GPU-VM stop/start cycles on MIAM-00111; a known PCIe/AER/riser condition has previously required a host reboot to recover.
- Preserve custom/patched vLLM environments. Do not `pip install -U vllm` into a known-good runtime.
- Experimental model stacks belong in versioned paths/containers/VMs.

## 4. Configuration and model truth

The live registry and verified runtime state are authoritative for current deployments.

Do not infer that an alias is local merely from its name. Verify provider/runtime/model identity.

When documenting a model deployment, capture:

- actual artifact/revision/checksum;
- runtime/image/commit;
- quantization;
- host/VM/site;
- context;
- TP/PP/DP/EP and relevant runtime flags;
- RAM/VRAM/hardware;
- route/alias;
- health and benchmark evidence.

## 5. History and rollback

Deployment history must make engineering work reproducible.

- `Load Configuration` is non-destructive.
- `Restore & Serve` is a separate confirmed operation.
- Failed experiments may be retained but hidden by default.
- Do not delete the last known-good revision merely because a newer model works.

## 6. Benchmark discipline

A performance number without deployment context is misleading.

Link benchmarks to exact deployment revisions and record at least:

- TTFT;
- output tokens/sec per request/agent;
- aggregate output tokens/sec;
- concurrency;
- prompt/output token counts;
- tested context;
- RAM/VRAM;
- host/hardware/runtime;
- errors;
- representative Hermes/tool-use status when relevant.

When comparing models, prefer same-node tests. When comparing nodes, use the same model/configuration first so hardware effects are visible.

## 7. Secrets and data

Never commit:

- `.env` files containing secrets;
- API keys/tokens;
- passwords;
- SSH/private keys;
- production database row dumps;
- customer-sensitive data;
- raw logs containing credentials.

Sanitize configuration into `.example` files. Run a secret scan before commit. If a secret appears in a tool transcript or repository candidate, stop and surface it for rotation/removal.

## 8. Git workflow

- Work on a dedicated branch.
- Branch format: `type/ticket-short-description`.
- Commit format: `type(scope): short description (#issue-id)`.
- Do not invent an issue ID; create/use an approved issue according to repository policy.
- Keep capture/bootstrapping changes reviewable.
- A human must approve before merge.

## 9. ADR policy

If a human explicitly made a significant architecture decision, record it as an ADR and surface it in the PR.

If the agent thinks a significant new decision is required, draft `Status: Proposed` and stop for human approval before treating it as accepted.

## 10. TDR policy

Use a TDR for intentional shortcuts, patched runtimes, manual recovery procedures, missing automation, known hardware/software limitations, or other accepted debt.

## 11. Validation

Run the exact project validation commands once captured/documented. At minimum for a Python service, consider syntax/import checks, tests, config parsing, migration/schema verification, and API smoke tests in a safe environment.

Report `Pass`, `Fail`, or `Not run` accurately. Never infer a passing test.

## 12. Stop and ask for human judgment when

- requirements conflict;
- an ACMS-facing contract would be invented or materially changed;
- a destructive production action is required outside the approved maintenance scope;
- credentials/access are required and unavailable;
- a security boundary changes;
- production tests fail unexpectedly;
- a new persistent model policy would be made without approved authority;
- the diff expands beyond the approved capture/documentation scope.

## 13. Handoff standard

At completion provide:

1. requirements addressed;
2. source/code captured and source locations;
3. files excluded and why;
4. changes made;
5. validation commands/results;
6. production impact (if any);
7. ADR/TDR status;
8. future-work items discovered;
9. known risks/unresolved questions;
10. exact branch, commit, PR, and recommended next action.

{"output": "\"\"\"Choose the target node for a NEW worker VM. Fail-closed.\n\n    Returns PlacementDecision; rejected=True when no node satisfies hard\n    filters (caller must NOT clone).\n    \"\"\"\n    s = get_settings()\n    total_workers = _worker_counts_by_node(session)\n    inference_workers = _inference_worker_counts_by_node(session)\n\n    scored: list[PlacementDecision] = []\n    for node in _candidate_nodes(session, provider):\n        reasons: list[str] = []\n        penalty = 0.0\n\n        if node == GENERAL_PREFERRED_NODE:\n            scored.append(PlacementDecision(node=node, score=100.0,\n                                            reasons=[\"general_preferred\"]))\n            continue\n\n        if node in INFERENCE_NODES:\n            # hard cap for INFERENCE-class workers; general workers get a\n            # large penalty instead (a GPU node hosting a dev worker is\n            # allowed but discouraged)\n            if runtime_class in INFERENCE_RUNTIME_CLASSES:\n                if inference_workers.get(node, 0) >= MAX_WORKERS_PER_INFERENCE_NODE:\n                    scored.append(PlacementDecision(node=node, score=0.0,\n                                                    rejected=True,\n                                                    rejection_reason=\"inference_node_at_worker_cap\"))\n                    continue\n            else:\n                penalty += 60.0\n                reasons.append(\"inference_node_penalty\")\n            score = 40.0 - penalty\n            scored.append(PlacementDecision(node=node, score=score, reasons=reasons))\n            continue\n\n        # any other healthy node\n        load_bonus = -2.0 * total_workers.get(node, 0)\n        scored.append(PlacementDecision(node=node, score=20.0 + load_bonus,\n                                        reasons=[\"generic\", f\"existing_workers={total_workers.get(node, 0)}\"]))\n\n    # deterministic tie-break: template node affinity LAST (clone + offline\n    # storage-migrate proven), then name\n    scored.sort(key=lambda d: (-d.score, d.node != template_node, d.node))\n    if not scored or all(d.rejected for d in scored):\n        return PlacementDecision(node=\"\", score=0.0, rejected=True,\n                                 rejection_reason=\"no_eligible_node\")\n    return scored[0]", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

Execution task now PRE-created before the bridge call (task id + COMPLETION PROTOCOL travel in the instruction; gated on ACMS_CALLBACK_BASE_URL); dedupe ledger moved from external_task_id (now = real A2A run id) to EXECUTION_DISPATCHED event metadata. **Live proof: dispatch → session auto-open → Hermes run completed (worker did NOT call back) → reconciler closed task from bridge run status → session CLOSED — zero manual close.** Reconciler also repairs orphaned OPEN sessions (task-already-terminal ordering) — found live, fixed in #46.\n8|- **GLM-fast worker ignored the in-instruction completion protocol** — treat the reconcile sweep as the working completion authority until a runtime-side callback hook exists (Hermes api-server has no run-completion webhook; a worker-side routine could POST it).\n9|- **Clone hygiene gate (LLM Mgr PR #59):** provisioner step 6 HYGIENE_GATE fail-closed (VMID/MAC/hostname/IP-uniqueness/netplan-reidentification/guest-identity from the clone-time ownership marker); failure ⇒ job FAILED, runtime ERROR, vmid NOT promoted, durable clone_hygiene_failed event for human disposal. Collector `insert_samples` uses it behind the flag (falls through to legacy executemany on adapter error). 312 samples/5min live; retention settings 90/730 read via ORM.\n12|- **ROLLUP HOUR-ALIGN BUG (PR #66, found via live verify FAIL 104 mismatches):** cmd_rollup window started at now-3h MID-HOUR → the oldest hour bucket upserted from a truncated row set, deficit PERMANENT (rollup 134 vs raw 142 per bucket). Fix: hour-aligned start. Live repair: re-aggregated 24h (576 buckets) → **VERIFY PASSED 0 failures**.
